- `d8d6`: Move to d6. Safe? (White Queen at c5 could take d6: `c5-d6` is a diagonal move. No, (d-c, 6-5) = (1, 1). Yes, it's a diagonal move. So `d8d6` is a blunder).
- `d8b6`: Blunder.
- `d8d5`: Blunder.
- `d8d4`: Safe? (c5 to d4 is (d-c, 4-5) = (1, -1). Yes, it's a diagonal. Blunder).
- `d8d3`: Safe? (c5 to d3 is (d-c, 3-5) = (1, -2). Not a queen move. Safe?)
- `d8d2`: Blunder (Bxd2).

So for the Queen, only `d8d7`, `d8c7` and maybe `d8d3` (but that's too far) are safe.

What about other moves?
`e7e6` is a very standard move. It prepares to develop the bishop at f8.
`b7b6` is too risky (Qxb6).
`e7e5` is possible.
Does `e7e5` attack anything? No.
Does it block anything? No.

Wait, let's look at the bishop at c8.
`c8e6`, `c8f5`, `c8g4`, `c8h3`.
Are these safe?
- `c8e6`: (e-c, 6-8) = (2, -2). Diagonal.
Is e6 attacked by anything?
White Queen at c5. `c5-e6` is (e-c, 6-5) = (2, 1). Not a queen move.
White Bishop at a4. `a4-e6` is (e-a, 6-4) = (4, 2). Not a bishop move.
So `c8e6` is safe.
- `c8f5`: (f-c, 5-8) = (3, -3). Diagonal.
Is f5 attacked?
White Queen at c5. `c5-f5` is (f-c, 5-5) = (3, 0). Yes, it's a horizontal move!
So `c8f5` is a blunder (Qxf5).
- `c8g4`: (g-c, 4-8) = (4, -4). Diagonal.
Is g4 attacked?
White Queen at c5. `c5-g4` is (g-c, 4-5) = (4, -1). No.
White Bishop at a4. `a4-g4` is (g-a, 4-4) = (6, 0). No.
So `c8g4` is safe.

Comparing `e7e6`, `e7e5`, `c8e6`, `c8g4`.
`e7e6` is a solid developing move.
`c8e6` is also developing.
Let's see if `e7e6` is better.
If Black plays `e7e6`, it prepares `d5` (a pawn move).

White's Queen is at c5.
If Black plays `e7e6`, the Queen at c5 is still there.
Is there any way to kick the Queen?
`d8b6` was a blunder.
`b7b6` is `Qxb6`.
What if Black plays `e7e5`?
Then `d8d5` is still a blunder.

Wait, I just noticed something.
Row 4 (Line 5): `♗ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘` (Bishop at a4)
Row 5 (Line 4): `♟ ⭘ ♕ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘` (Pawn at a5, Queen at c5)
Wait, the pawn at a5 is attacking the Bishop at a4?
Let's check:
Pawn at a5. White Bishop at a4.
Pawns attack diagonally.
Black pawn at a5 attacks b4.
White Bishop is at a4.
So the pawn at a5 is NOT attacking the Bishop at a4.

Okay, let's go with a solid move. `e7e6` seems like a good choice to start developing.
Or maybe `e7e5` to take more center.
Actually, `e7e6` is safer and prepares the bishop.
